*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical issue in vision-language understanding: the ability to perform multi-step reasoning. Improving this capability is essential for developing more robust and reliable AI systems. The results demonstrate that the proposed framework effectively enhances reasoning accuracy on challenging VQA datasets.  The quantitative improvements over baseline methods are significant, particularly on VQA-Introspect, and the ablation studies provide insights into the impact of the number of sub-questions on the accuracy.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Image-aware question generation:** Grounding the question generation process within a VLM enables the creation of more informative and relevant sub-questions.
    *   **Iterative approach:**  The iterative generation of sub-questions allows the model to progressively refine its understanding of the image and the question.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper includes experiments on two challenging datasets, ablation studies on the number of sub-questions, and qualitative examples to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The method uses a fully open architecture which supports reproducibility and inspection of intermediate steps.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Answerer accuracy:** The paper acknowledges that the accuracy of the Answerer can limit the overall performance. The reliance on a VLM for answering sub-questions may introduce noise due to imperfect visual understanding.
    *   **Computational cost:** The iterative process of generating and answering sub-questions can be computationally expensive compared to single-step reasoning. This is not explicitly addressed in the paper.
    *   **Limited comparison:** The paper references previous methods that use ChatGPT for question generation, but does not directly compare to them in quantitative experiments. It is difficult to judge the relative performance of the proposed method versus an end-to-end approach that incorporates a LLM such as ChatGPT.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in multimodal reasoning by demonstrating the effectiveness of iterative, image-aware self-questioning. The framework provides a solid foundation for developing more sophisticated reasoning systems.  The emphasis on explainability and control through the open-source approach is a positive step.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of 7.

*   The paper does build upon existing ideas, but offers a significant improvement by grounding the self-questioning within a VLM and adding an instruction-tuned component. It is a substantial, practical improvement that is thoroughly tested.
*   The results are compelling and demonstrate improved performance compared to baseline methods and the design also offers the benefit of transparency and reproducibility, which is essential for academic research.
*   However, the reliance on a VLM for both question generation and answering introduces potential limitations, and the computational cost isn't fully addressed. The evaluation also includes limitations such as lack of a direct comparision with state-of-the-art LLM-based models, and lack of an exploration of open-endedness for A-OKVQA.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Semantic Edge-Cloud Communication for Real-Time Urban Traffic Surveillance with ViT and LLMs over Mobile Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21259v1)
- **Authors**: Murat Arda Onsu, Poonam Lohan, Burak Kantarci, Aisha Syed, Matthew Andrews, Sean Kennedy
- **Abstract**: Real-time urban traffic surveillance is vital for Intelligent Transportation Systems (ITS) to ensure road safety, optimize traffic flow, track vehicle trajectories, and prevent collisions in smart cities. Deploying edge cameras across urban environments is a standard practice for monitoring road conditions. However, integrating these with intelligent models requires a robust understanding of dynamic traffic scenarios and a responsive interface for user interaction. Although multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs) can interpret traffic images and generate informative responses, their deployment on edge devices is infeasible due to high computational demands. Therefore, LLM inference must occur on the cloud, necessitating visual data transmission from edge to cloud, a process hindered by limited bandwidth, leading to potential delays that compromise real-time performance. To address this challenge, we propose a semantic communication framework that significantly reduces transmission overhead. Our method involves detecting Regions of Interest (RoIs) using YOLOv11, cropping relevant image segments, and converting them into compact embedding vectors using a Vision Transformer (ViT). These embeddings are then transmitted to the cloud, where an image decoder reconstructs the cropped images. The reconstructed images are processed by a multimodal LLM to generate traffic condition descriptions. This approach achieves a 99.9% reduction in data transmission size while maintaining an LLM response accuracy of 89% for reconstructed cropped images, compared to 93% accuracy with original cropped images. Our results demonstrate the efficiency and practicality of ViT and LLM-assisted edge-cloud semantic communication for real-time traffic surveillance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a semantic communication framework for real-time urban traffic surveillance using a combination of YOLOv11 for object detection, Vision Transformer (ViT) for feature embedding, a custom image decoder using transposed CNNs, and a fine-tuned multimodal Large Language Model (LLM) (LLaVA 1.5). The goal is to reduce the amount of data transmitted from edge cameras to a cloud server for traffic analysis. YOLOv11 identifies Regions of Interest (RoIs) containing vehicles. These RoIs are cropped, and ViT converts them into compact embedding vectors.  These embeddings are transmitted to the cloud where the image decoder reconstructs the images. The reconstructed images are fed to the LLM to generate traffic condition descriptions. The authors demonstrate significant data reduction (99.9%) using the embedding vectors while maintaining reasonable LLM response accuracy (89% compared to 93% with original cropped images). The paper presents numerical results on memory savings, MSE, LPIPS, and LLM response accuracy under different channel conditions and using different quantization and modulation schemes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of YOLOv11, ViT, a custom CNN-based image decoder, and LLaVA 1.5 in a semantic communication framework for traffic surveillance has some novelty. Semantic communication for ITS is not entirely new, but this specific architecture and evaluation across different channel conditions is a contribution. The integrated use of object detection, image compression via ViT embeddings, image reconstruction, and LLM-based interpretation is a worthwhile engineering approach. The emphasis on reducing the data transmission overhead via feature embedding, particularly in the context of resource-constrained edge devices, is relevant and significant. However, existing works use LLMs with visual transformers that dynamically identify and prioritize the most informative parts of the input data via an attention mechanism. This paper should distinguish itself more clearly from attention mask-guided visual transformers.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in intelligent transportation systems: efficiently processing and transmitting visual data from edge devices. The demonstrated 99.9% reduction in data transmission size is a significant achievement that could enable real-time traffic monitoring in bandwidth-constrained environments. The framework's ability to maintain reasonable LLM response accuracy despite the extreme data reduction makes it a practical solution. The comprehensive evaluation, including MSE, LPIPS, and accuracy analysis across various channel conditions (SNR), adds credibility to the proposed approach. The use of the Quanser Interactive Lab for generating realistic traffic scenarios is also a strength.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined architecture and methodology.
    *   Significant data reduction with reasonable LLM accuracy.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation.
    *   Use of a realistic simulation environment (Quanser).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty could be more clearly articulated by contrasting with attention mask-guided transformer approaches.
    *   The impact of image quality degradation on LLM's performance needs more elaborate discussion. LPIPS score, although useful, may not fully capture the semantic fidelity necessary for precise traffic analysis.
    *   The evaluation focuses on simulated data. Real-world data validation is necessary to assess the framework's robustness to noise, weather conditions, and other environmental factors.
    *   Limited variety of text-based queries used for evaluating the multimodal LLM.
    *   Although inference time is mentioned, a more detailed analysis of the end-to-end latency is necessary, particularly in edge-cloud environments.
    *   Performance improvements of YOLOv11 over other available object detection is not mentioned, and it has a crucial impact on ROI extraction. The training methodology of YOLOv11 model should be mentioned as well.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future edge-cloud ITS architectures. The proposed framework could be adapted for other applications requiring efficient transmission and analysis of visual data, such as environmental monitoring, security surveillance, and autonomous robotics. The paper's findings could encourage further research into the use of semantic communication and LLMs for real-time data analysis in resource-constrained environments.
*   **Real world performance:** The paper utilizes simulated data, hence the model performance under real-world circumstances has not been addressed.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable engineering solution to a relevant problem. The combination of techniques and the demonstrated data reduction are significant contributions. However, the lack of real-world validation, and slightly limited query variety holds it back from a higher score. The degree of novelty relative to existing semantic communication techniques using attention is also a factor. Although not groundbreaking, the paper offers a practical and well-evaluated framework that could be valuable to researchers and practitioners in the field of intelligent transportation systems.

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Un-Doubling Diffusion: LLM-guided Disambiguation of Homonym Duplication
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21262v1)
- **Authors**: Evgeny Kaskov, Elizaveta Petrova, Petr Surovtsev, Anna Kostikova, Ilya Mistiurin, Alexander Kapitanov, Alexander Nagaev
- **Abstract**: Homonyms are words with identical spelling but distinct meanings, which pose challenges for many generative models. When a homonym appears in a prompt, diffusion models may generate multiple senses of the word simultaneously, which is known as homonym duplication. This issue is further complicated by an Anglocentric bias, which includes an additional translation step before the text-to-image model pipeline. As a result, even words that are not homonymous in the original language may become homonyms and lose their meaning after translation into English. In this paper, we introduce a method for measuring duplication rates and conduct evaluations of different diffusion models using both automatic evaluation utilizing Vision-Language Models (VLM) and human evaluation. Additionally, we investigate methods to mitigate the homonym duplication problem through prompt expansion, demonstrating that this approach also effectively reduces duplication related to Anglocentric bias. The code for the automatic evaluation pipeline is publicly available.
- **Summary**: This paper addresses the problem of homonym duplication in text-to-image diffusion models, where a single word with multiple meanings can lead to the generation of images containing multiple senses of the word simultaneously. The authors introduce a method for measuring duplication rates using both automatic evaluation (with Vision-Language Models, VLMs) and human evaluation. They create a homonym benchmark and evaluate several diffusion models. Furthermore, they investigate using LLM-guided prompt expansion to mitigate the homonym duplication problem, showing that this approach also reduces duplication related to Anglocentric bias (where translation from other languages into English can create artificial homonyms).

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a known and documented issue in generative models - the difficulty in handling polysemy and homonymy. While the problem itself isn't new, the paper makes a worthwhile contribution by:

1.  **Quantifying the Problem:**  The creation of a homonym benchmark and the development of automatic and human evaluation pipelines allow for systematic measurement and comparison of homonym duplication across different diffusion models. This moves the understanding of the problem beyond anecdotal evidence. However, the benchmark's size (171 homonyms) could be a limiting factor, especially if finer-grained analysis is desired in the future.

2.  **Addressing Anglocentric Bias:** The paper identifies and investigates the specific challenge of "artificial homonyms" created during translation into English. This is a significant observation, as it highlights how the dominance of English in training data can negatively impact performance for other languages. The experiments demonstrating the mitigation of this bias via prompt expansion are valuable.

3.  **LLM-guided Prompt Expansion:**  The use of LLMs to expand prompts and reduce ambiguity is a clever approach. The results demonstrating a reduction in duplication rates, both with and without translation, provide empirical evidence for the effectiveness of this technique.  The implementation details of the LLM prompt are a plus for reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Evaluation Methodology:**  The VLM-as-a-judge approach is not entirely novel, as the paper itself acknowledges similar approaches in prior work. While the authors apply it effectively to the specific problem of homonym duplication, the evaluation methodology doesn't introduce groundbreaking innovation. The reliance on specific VLMs and their potential biases is also a limitation. The correlation of Automatic and Human is also not very strong which leads to the question: is the VLM "seeing" what the human sees?
*   **Scope of LLM Integration:** While LLM-guided prompt expansion shows promising results, the paper only explores it with a single diffusion model (Pixart Alpha). Further exploration across different models is needed to determine the generalizability of this approach. Similarly, the experiments only explore one LLM for prompt expansion.

*   **Proper Name Bias Mitigation:**  The paper notes a "proper name bias", but doesn't explore ways to mitigate the problem.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its comprehensive analysis of homonym duplication in diffusion models, particularly its quantification of the problem, its identification of the Anglocentric bias component, and its demonstration of a practical mitigation strategy using LLM-guided prompt expansion. The publicly available evaluation pipeline adds to the value of the work. While some components lack groundbreaking novelty, the combination of insights and methods contributes to a better understanding and management of lexical ambiguity in image generation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper builds upon existing knowledge, adds useful quantitative analysis, offers a practical solution for mitigating homonym duplication (including translation-related issues), and provides a public resource for future research. Although the individual components might not be revolutionary, the study's overall contribution is solid. The scope of the study can be increased by using more LLMs and Diffusiton models in prompt expansion to further generalize the findings.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMR1: Enhancing Multimodal Reasoning with Variance-Aware Sampling and Open Resources
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21268v1)
- **Authors**: Sicong Leng, Jing Wang, Jiaxi Li, Hao Zhang, Zhiqiang Hu, Boqiang Zhang, Yuming Jiang, Hang Zhang, Xin Li, Lidong Bing, Deli Zhao, Wei Lu, Yu Rong, Aixin Sun, Shijian Lu
- **Abstract**: Large multimodal reasoning models have achieved rapid progress, but their advancement is constrained by two major limitations: the absence of open, large-scale, high-quality long chain-of-thought (CoT) data, and the instability of reinforcement learning (RL) algorithms in post-training. Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), the standard framework for RL fine-tuning, is prone to gradient vanishing when reward variance is low, which weakens optimization signals and impairs convergence. This work makes three contributions: (1) We propose Variance-Aware Sampling (VAS), a data selection strategy guided by Variance Promotion Score (VPS) that combines outcome variance and trajectory diversity to promote reward variance and stabilize policy optimization. (2) We release large-scale, carefully curated resources containing ~1.6M long CoT cold-start data and ~15k RL QA pairs, designed to ensure quality, difficulty, and diversity, along with a fully reproducible end-to-end training codebase. (3) We open-source a family of multimodal reasoning models in multiple scales, establishing standardized baselines for the community. Experiments across mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of both the curated data and the proposed VAS. Comprehensive ablation studies and analyses provide further insight into the contributions of each component. In addition, we theoretically establish that reward variance lower-bounds the expected policy gradient magnitude, with VAS serving as a practical mechanism to realize this guarantee. Our code, data, and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/LengSicong/MMR1.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MMR1: ENHANCING MULTIMODAL REASONING WITH VARIANCE-AWARE SAMPLING AND OPEN RESOURCES":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses two key limitations hindering progress in multimodal reasoning: the lack of open, high-quality, large-scale chain-of-thought (CoT) datasets and the instability of reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning, particularly the gradient vanishing problem in Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO). The authors propose Variance-Aware Sampling (VAS), a data selection strategy guided by a Variance Promotion Score (VPS) that promotes reward variance and stabilizes policy optimization. They release a large-scale curated dataset with long CoT data and RL QA pairs, along with open-source multimodal reasoning models and a training codebase. Experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of both the curated data and VAS, and theoretical justifications are provided for the proposed approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The idea of Variance-Aware Sampling (VAS) to combat gradient vanishing in GRPO is potentially novel. While prior work acknowledges the issue, the proposed method offers a specific and theoretically grounded data sampling strategy. The theoretical analysis, demonstrating that reward variance provides a lower bound on the policy gradient magnitude, is a strong point.
    *   The curation and release of a large-scale, high-quality multimodal CoT dataset is also a significant contribution, filling a gap in the field. While dataset curation is not fundamentally a new concept, the scale, quality controls, and attention to difficulty/diversity, as described, add value.
    *   The open-sourcing of multimodal models and a reproducible training codebase is a valuable service to the community and facilitates further research.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The gradient vanishing problem in RL fine-tuning is a fundamental challenge, and the VAS approach offers a promising solution. If the empirical results generalize across various tasks and model architectures, it could significantly improve the stability and effectiveness of RL fine-tuning for multimodal reasoning.
    *   The released dataset provides a valuable resource for training and evaluating multimodal reasoning models, potentially accelerating progress in the field. Having a common benchmark and training data source encourages reproducibility and fair comparisons between different approaches.
    *   The open-sourced models serve as useful baselines for the community, allowing researchers to build upon existing work and focus on novel contributions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides theoretical justification for the proposed VAS approach, linking reward variance to the expected policy gradient magnitude. This adds credibility and provides a principled foundation for the method.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results on mathematical reasoning benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of VAS and the curated dataset. Ablation studies provide further insight into the contributions of OVS and TDS.
    *   **Comprehensive Resources:** The release of a large-scale dataset, open-source models, and a reproducible codebase is a major strength, promoting reproducibility and enabling further research in the field.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations it addresses (lack of data, GRPO instability), making the contributions easier to appreciate.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The empirical evaluation is primarily focused on mathematical reasoning benchmarks. It is important to assess the generalizability of VAS to other multimodal reasoning tasks and domains.
    *   **Incremental Advance:**  While VAS seems to be effective within the context of GRPO fine-tuning, its novelty relative to existing variance reduction techniques in RL needs further clarification. A more thorough comparison to other approaches for stabilizing RL training would strengthen the paper. The actual value is that they seem to find a smart way of sampling, which then leads to variance-aware training.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by providing a solution to the gradient vanishing problem in RL fine-tuning for multimodal reasoning.
    *   The released dataset and open-source models will likely become valuable resources for the community, facilitating further research and development in this area.
    *   The VAS approach could be extended to other RL settings and potentially generalized to other types of instability in training large models.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**.  The paper addresses an important and challenging problem in multimodal reasoning and presents a potentially novel solution with strong theoretical backing and empirical validation.  The release of valuable resources is a major contribution.  The main drawbacks are the limited scope of the evaluation (primarily mathematical reasoning) and the reliance on the GRPO framework, meaning a more generalized claim cannot be made. While the work is significant, there is some level of incrementality and it doesn't revolutionize the landscape, the theoretical justification, empirical success, and resource contribution are still enough to give it a high score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLMTrace: A Corpus for Classification and Fine-Grained Localization of AI-Written Text
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21269v1)
- **Authors**: Irina Tolstykh, Aleksandra Tsybina, Sergey Yakubson, Maksim Kuprashevich
- **Abstract**: The widespread use of human-like text from Large Language Models (LLMs) necessitates the development of robust detection systems. However, progress is limited by a critical lack of suitable training data; existing datasets are often generated with outdated models, are predominantly in English, and fail to address the increasingly common scenario of mixed human-AI authorship. Crucially, while some datasets address mixed authorship, none provide the character-level annotations required for the precise localization of AI-generated segments within a text. To address these gaps, we introduce LLMTrace, a new large-scale, bilingual (English and Russian) corpus for AI-generated text detection. Constructed using a diverse range of modern proprietary and open-source LLMs, our dataset is designed to support two key tasks: traditional full-text binary classification (human vs. AI) and the novel task of AI-generated interval detection, facilitated by character-level annotations. We believe LLMTrace will serve as a vital resource for training and evaluating the next generation of more nuanced and practical AI detection models. The project page is available at \href{https://sweetdream779.github.io/LLMTrace-info/}{iitolstykh/LLMTrace}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LLMTRACE: A CORPUS FOR CLASSIFICATION AND FINE-GRAINED LOCALIZATION OF AI-WRITTEN TEXT":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLMTrace, a new large-scale, bilingual (English and Russian) corpus for AI-generated text detection. The corpus is designed to address limitations in existing datasets, including their reliance on outdated LLMs, primary focus on English, and lack of character-level annotations for precise AI-generated segment localization in mixed human-AI authorship scenarios. LLMTrace facilitates two tasks: full-text binary classification (human vs. AI) and AI-generated interval detection through character-level annotations. The corpus is constructed using a diverse range of modern LLMs and involves complex generation scenarios and manual refinement to create subtle and sophisticated examples. The authors also present baseline experiments to demonstrate the utility of the dataset for these tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The most significant aspect of novelty lies in the character-level annotations for AI-generated segments within mixed authorship texts. While other datasets address the classification of human vs. AI text, and some address *detection* of human-vs-AI boundaries, few provide the fine-grained *localization* necessary for advanced AI detection methods. The bilingual nature (English and Russian) and the use of a broad suite of modern LLMs contribute to its novelty. However, other datasets covering English are already available. The gap-filling methodology, while well executed, is not inherently novel in its concept, but the *scale* of its implementation at this granular level combined with manual refinements is a crucial element. The combination of these elements is highly novel.

*   **Significance:** LLMTrace has significant potential to advance the field of AI-generated text detection. The absence of resources for fine-grained localization of AI text has hampered progress in developing more nuanced and practical detection models. By providing a large-scale, diverse, and well-annotated corpus, LLMTrace empowers researchers to train and evaluate more sophisticated detectors. Its importance is also underscored by the increasing prevalence of mixed human-AI authorship in various contexts. Its significance will depend on its widespread adoption by the AI-detection research community. It is also difficult to assess its significance without access to the dataset.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Character-level Annotations:** This is the main strength, enabling a new task and facilitating the development of more accurate detection models.
    *   **Bilingual Support:** Addressing the lack of resources for non-English languages, particularly Russian, is a major asset.
    *   **Diverse LLM Suite:** Using a wide array of LLMs (proprietary and open-source) helps avoid overfitting to the artifacts of a single model family.
    *   **Complex Generation Scenarios:** The combination of automated and manual methods ensures the creation of challenging and realistic examples of mixed human-AI authorship.
    *   **Rigorous Analysis:** The authors thoroughly analyze the characteristics of the dataset and demonstrate its high quality and complexity.
    *   **Baseline experiments:** Showing clear improvements for both tasks.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper does not cover the data licenses used for its data sources for human-authored data. This is a common limitation in many paper.
    *   The data size for detection is limited (79.342 including human, AI, and mixed texts, with the mixed subset noted in parentheses), which could limit the model.

*   **Potential Influence:** LLMTrace can serve as a benchmark for evaluating AI-generated text detection models and inspire the development of new detection techniques. The character-level annotations can be utilized for training models that not only detect AI-generated text but also identify its exact location within a text, facilitating applications such as plagiarism detection, content moderation, and fake news mitigation.

*   **Score Justification:** LLMTrace addresses a crucial gap in the field of AI-generated text detection, especially focusing on fine-grained AI-generated interval detection and bilingual resource. The paper demonstrates that the methodology used for creating the dataset is sound, its structural complexity and topological features are well-measured by comparison with other human datasets, and that the data is effective to improve on previous detection methods. It is well-written, well-motivated and presented clearly with experiments and analyses.

    However, its limited detection data size and lack of data licenses are limitations. Considering the limitations of the other datasets, and the contributions made by the proposed approach, a score of 8 is appropriate.

**Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Does FLUX Already Know How to Perform Physically Plausible Image Composition?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21278v1)
- **Authors**: Shilin Lu, Zhuming Lian, Zihan Zhou, Shaocong Zhang, Chen Zhao, Adams Wai-Kin Kong
- **Abstract**: Image composition aims to seamlessly insert a user-specified object into a new scene, but existing models struggle with complex lighting (e.g., accurate shadows, water reflections) and diverse, high-resolution inputs. Modern text-to-image diffusion models (e.g., SD3.5, FLUX) already encode essential physical and resolution priors, yet lack a framework to unleash them without resorting to latent inversion, which often locks object poses into contextually inappropriate orientations, or brittle attention surgery. We propose SHINE, a training-free framework for Seamless, High-fidelity Insertion with Neutralized Errors. SHINE introduces manifold-steered anchor loss, leveraging pretrained customization adapters (e.g., IP-Adapter) to guide latents for faithful subject representation while preserving background integrity. Degradation-suppression guidance and adaptive background blending are proposed to further eliminate low-quality outputs and visible seams. To address the lack of rigorous benchmarks, we introduce ComplexCompo, featuring diverse resolutions and challenging conditions such as low lighting, strong illumination, intricate shadows, and reflective surfaces. Experiments on ComplexCompo and DreamEditBench show state-of-the-art performance on standard metrics (e.g., DINOv2) and human-aligned scores (e.g., DreamSim, ImageReward, VisionReward). Code and benchmark will be publicly available upon publication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SHINE, a novel training-free framework for physically plausible image composition using pre-trained text-to-image diffusion models. SHINE aims to seamlessly insert objects into new scenes, addressing limitations of existing methods related to lighting realism, resolution rigidity, and pose constraints. The framework incorporates three key innovations: (1) Manifold-Steered Anchor (MSA) loss, guiding latents for faithful subject representation while maintaining background integrity; (2) Degradation-Suppression Guidance (DSG), steering sampling away from low-quality distributions; and (3) Adaptive Background Blending (ABB), eliminating visible seams along mask boundaries. The paper also introduces a new benchmark, ComplexCompo, designed to evaluate performance across diverse resolutions and challenging lighting conditions. Experiments on ComplexCompo and DreamEditBench demonstrate SHINE's state-of-the-art performance compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its synergistic combination of MSA loss, DSG, and ABB within a training-free framework for image composition. While each component individually builds upon existing concepts (e.g., MSA relates to manifold learning and score distillation sampling; DSG is inspired by negative prompting), their integration and application to the specific problem of physically plausible image composition represent a significant advancement. The introduction of ComplexCompo is also valuable.

*   **Significance:** SHINE tackles a crucial problem in image editing and generative AI: the ability to create visually coherent and physically plausible composites. The training-free nature of the approach is significant as it leverages the inherent priors encoded in powerful pre-trained diffusion models without the need for costly and potentially biased fine-tuning on synthetic data. The performance gains demonstrated on both existing and the newly introduced benchmark showcase the potential of the framework to advance the field. However, the reliance on specific adapters (IP-Adapter) may limit widespread adoption if these adapters become less relevant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-free approach:** Eliminates the need for large, high-quality datasets and fine-tuning.
    *   **Physically plausible composition:** Addresses key challenges related to lighting, shadows, and reflections.
    *   **Synergistic design:** The integration of MSA, DSG, and ABB creates a robust framework.
    *   **New benchmark:** ComplexCompo provides a challenging evaluation environment.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** SHINE outperforms existing methods on multiple benchmarks.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on pre-trained adapters:** The MSA loss depends on the availability and performance of customization adapters like IP-Adapter, potentially limiting generalizability if these adapters are unavailable or not performing well.
    *   **Complexity of implementation:** While training-free, implementing SHINE requires careful tuning of hyperparameters and integration with existing diffusion pipelines, which may pose a barrier to entry for some users.
    *   **Limited quantitative assessment of ABB:** The paper acknowledges that quantitative metrics may not fully capture the benefits of Adaptive Background Blending.
    *   **No visual differences between experiments:** While the experimental results provide quantitative data, it's difficult to perceive substantial visual improvements compared to the baseline by simply looking at the images, as their differences are relatively minor.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in image composition and generative AI. The training-free framework offers a promising direction for leveraging pre-trained models for complex image editing tasks. The introduction of ComplexCompo should stimulate research in more challenging and realistic image composition scenarios. However, the rapid progress in generative AI suggests that this paper will quickly become outdated.

Score: 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of image composition. While the individual components may not be entirely novel, their integration into a training-free framework is both innovative and impactful. The experimental results are compelling, and the introduction of ComplexCompo addresses a gap in existing benchmarks. The reliance on external adapters and implementation complexity temper the impact somewhat.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### It's Not You, It's Clipping: A Soft Trust-Region via Probability Smoothing for LLM RL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21282v1)
- **Authors**: Madeleine Dwyer, Adam Sobey, Adriane Chapman
- **Abstract**: Training large language models (LLMs) with reinforcement learning (RL) methods such as PPO and GRPO commonly relies on ratio clipping to stabilise updates. While effective at preventing instability, clipping discards information and introduces gradient discontinuities. We propose Probability Smoothing Policy Optimisation (PSPO), which smooths the current policy's probabilities toward the old (behaviour) policy before computing the importance ratio, analogous to label smoothing. Unlike clipping, PSPO preserves gradient signal, while interpolation toward the old policy creates a soft trust region that discourages large, destabilising updates, with formal guarantees. We instantiate PSPO within GRPO (GR-PSPO) and fine-tune Qwen2.5-0.5B and Qwen2.5-1.5B on GSM8K, evaluating on GSM8K test and the cross-dataset generalisation on SVAMP, ASDiv, and MATH-500. Relative to unclipped GRPO (single iteration; no data reuse, ratio always = 1), GR-PSPO achieves similar performance but improves the reasoning leading to clearer and more concise responses which are more logical. Compared to clipped GRPO, GR-PSPO substantially improves performance both the 0.5B and 1.5B models, with a boost of over 20% on GSM8K (39.7% vs. 17.6% for 0.5B, 59.4% vs. 37.8% for 1.5B).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Probability Smoothing Policy Optimisation (PSPO), an alternative to ratio clipping in reinforcement learning (RL) for fine-tuning large language models (LLMs). Instead of truncating the importance ratio, PSPO smooths the current policy's probabilities towards the old (behavior) policy before computing the ratio.  This approach aims to preserve gradient information, create a soft trust region that discourages overly large updates, and avoid gradient discontinuities introduced by clipping. The authors instantiate PSPO within Group Relative Policy Optimisation (GRPO), calling it GR-PSPO, and evaluate it on mathematical reasoning tasks (GSM8K, ASDiv, SVAMP, MATH-500) using Qwen2.5 models. Results demonstrate that GR-PSPO outperforms clipped GRPO and exhibits improved response quality (clarity, coherence, constraint adherence).  It achieves comparable performance to unclipped GRPO but with more stable training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of probability smoothing for policy optimization, inspired by label smoothing, is relatively novel in the context of LLM reinforcement learning. While smoothing techniques are known in supervised learning and other RL contexts, the application and demonstration within the GRPO framework for LLMs, alongside the formal guarantees regarding gradient preservation and soft trust region formation, adds a distinct contribution. The concept of using the *behavior* policy for smoothing, specifically, is a clever way to build a trust region.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a well-known problem in LLM RL: the instability introduced by aggressive policy updates and the shortcomings of ratio clipping.  PSPO offers a practical and potentially more stable alternative. The empirical results, demonstrating improved performance and response quality compared to standard clipped GRPO, support the claim that PSPO is a viable and perhaps superior method. The fact that PSPO is computationally simple, requiring minimal overhead, enhances its practical appeal. However, its similar quantitive perfomance with GRPO-noclip does make it somewhat less of a practical advance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of ratio clipping.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:**  The connection to label smoothing is intuitive, and the idea of creating a soft trust region anchored on the old policy is well-explained.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides formal guarantees (Lemmas and Propositions) to support the claims about gradient preservation, soft trust region formation, and overconfidence regularization.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experimental results support the effectiveness of GR-PSPO, especially regarding improved accuracy and response quality.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is primarily limited to mathematical reasoning tasks. It would be beneficial to assess the effectiveness of PSPO on a broader range of tasks, particularly those involving more subjective or continuous reward signals.
    *   **Modest Performance Gains over No Clipping:** The paper itself notes that the performance of GR-PSPO is comparable to GRPO-noclip (especially on the smaller model). This raises the question of whether the added complexity of PSPO justifies its adoption, especially when considering implementation and tuning overhead.
    *   **Model Size and Architectures:** The experiments are conducted on relatively smaller models (0.5B and 1.5B parameters).  It's unclear how well PSPO will scale to larger, more complex LLMs.
    *   **Lack of Hyperparameter Sensitivity Analysis:** While the authors mention hyperparameter tuning, they do not provide a detailed analysis of the sensitivity of PSPO's performance to the smoothing strength (alpha).

*   **Potential Influence:** PSPO could potentially influence the design of more stable and efficient RL algorithms for LLMs. It may encourage researchers to explore other smoothing techniques and to move away from hard clipping mechanisms.  The insights into the relationship between probability smoothing and implicit divergence control are valuable.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM RL. The idea of PSPO addresses a crucial problem: unstable training due to clipping and aggressive updates. The theoretical justification, coupled with the empirical validation showing performance improvements over clipped GRPO and quality improvements overall, strengthens the impact of the work. The main limitation that holds back the paper is that it does not show much quantitive gain compared to GRPO-noclip (as the authors themselves admit), so it remains to be seen if PSPO has practical significance. Considering these factors,

### Bounds of Chain-of-Thought Robustness: Reasoning Steps, Embed Norms, and Beyond
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21284v1)
- **Authors**: Dingzirui Wang, Xuanliang Zhang, Keyan Xu, Qingfu Zhu, Wanxiang Che, Yang Deng
- **Abstract**: Existing research indicates that the output of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) is significantly affected by input perturbations. Although many methods aim to mitigate such impact by optimizing prompts, a theoretical explanation of how these perturbations influence CoT outputs remains an open area of research. This gap limits our in-depth understanding of how input perturbations propagate during the reasoning process and hinders further improvements in prompt optimization methods. Therefore, in this paper, we theoretically analyze the effect of input perturbations on the fluctuation of CoT outputs. We first derive an upper bound for input perturbations under the condition that the output fluctuation is within an acceptable range, based on which we prove that: (i) This upper bound is positively correlated with the number of reasoning steps in the CoT; (ii) Even an infinitely long reasoning process cannot eliminate the impact of input perturbations. We then apply these conclusions to the Linear Self-Attention (LSA) model, which can be viewed as a simplified version of the Transformer. For the LSA model, we prove that the upper bound for input perturbation is negatively correlated with the norms of the input embedding and hidden state vectors. To validate this theoretical analysis, we conduct experiments on three mainstream datasets and four mainstream models. The experimental results align with our theoretical analysis, empirically demonstrating the correctness of our findings.
- **Summary**: This paper theoretically analyzes the effect of input perturbations on the output of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in large language models (LLMs). The authors derive an upper bound for input perturbations under the condition that the output fluctuation is within an acceptable range. They prove that this upper bound is positively correlated with the number of reasoning steps in CoT and that even an infinitely long reasoning process cannot eliminate the impact of input perturbations. The analysis is then applied to a Linear Self-Attention (LSA) model, where it's proven that the upper bound for input perturbation is negatively correlated with the norms of the input embedding and hidden state vectors. Experiments on mainstream datasets and models are conducted to validate the theoretical analysis. The experimental results align with the theoretical analysis, empirically demonstrating the correctness of their findings, and improved prompt optimization is proposed using the theoretical framework.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the understanding of CoT robustness by providing a theoretical framework for analyzing the impact of input perturbations. While previous work has identified CoT's sensitivity to input, this paper provides a formal explanation of *why* and *how* perturbations propagate through the reasoning process. The derivation of the upper bound for input perturbation and its relationship to reasoning steps and embedding norms is novel. The application to the LSA model is a useful simplification to illustrate the theoretical findings.

**Strengths:**

*   **Theoretical grounding:** The paper provides a much-needed theoretical framework for understanding CoT robustness, moving beyond empirical observations.
*   **Clear conclusions:** The key findings regarding the relationship between reasoning steps, embedding norms, and robustness are clearly presented and supported by the theoretical analysis.
*   **Empirical validation:** The experiments provide empirical support for the theoretical claims, strengthening the validity of the work.
*   **Prompt Optimization:** Provides insight from their theoretical analysis for prompt optimization.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Assumptions:** The analysis relies on the assumption of Lipschitz continuity, which might not hold in all cases for real-world LLMs. The paper states the assumption that y < 1, which may also not hold in every instance of model training, as the output fluctuation may not converge.
*   **LSA Model Simplification:** While LSA serves as a simplified case study, its linear nature might not fully capture the complexities of real-world Transformers and their non-linear activation functions. The paper does address this limitation to a degree by discussing non-linearities in Appendix B.1.
*   **Limited Exploration of Parameters Gamma and Eta:** The conclusion states "In particular, a key next step is to systematically examine how the parameters Γ and η in Theorem 2 influence input robustness, which could also inform the design of more resilient large reasoning models." This weakness limits the degree to which the authors were able to provide an understanding of CoT robustness.

**Significance:**

The paper's theoretical analysis can help guide the development of more robust CoT prompting strategies and inform the design of LLMs that are less sensitive to input variations. The work opens up new avenues for research on the internal mechanisms of CoT reasoning and can potentially lead to more effective prompt optimization techniques. The proposed prompt optimization method seems promising.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a significant contribution in formalizing CoT robustness with theoretical analysis. The conclusions, backed by experimental data, increase understanding of how the number of reasoning steps and input/hidden state embeddings directly impact the robustness of models. While the use of LSA and other assumptions represent simplifications, these enable tractable analysis and valuable insights. Although the paper includes a prompt optimization method based on theoretical insights, the limited exploration of parameters Γ and η does hamper their understanding of CoT robustness to a limited degree. The paper introduces valuable work that can further be used to improve robustness of CoT and LLMs. Considering its contribution and potential impact, a score of 7 is assigned.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### VC-Agent: An Interactive Agent for Customized Video Dataset Collection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21291v1)
- **Authors**: Yidan Zhang, Mutian Xu, Yiming Hao, Kun Zhou, Jiahao Chang, Xiaoqiang Liu, Pengfei Wan, Hongbo Fu, Xiaoguang Han
- **Abstract**: Facing scaling laws, video data from the internet becomes increasingly important. However, collecting extensive videos that meet specific needs is extremely labor-intensive and time-consuming. In this work, we study the way to expedite this collection process and propose VC-Agent, the first interactive agent that is able to understand users' queries and feedback, and accordingly retrieve/scale up relevant video clips with minimal user input. Specifically, considering the user interface, our agent defines various user-friendly ways for the user to specify requirements based on textual descriptions and confirmations. As for agent functions, we leverage existing multi-modal large language models to connect the user's requirements with the video content. More importantly, we propose two novel filtering policies that can be updated when user interaction is continually performed. Finally, we provide a new benchmark for personalized video dataset collection, and carefully conduct the user study to verify our agent's usage in various real scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of our agent for customized video dataset collection. Project page: https://allenyidan.github.io/vcagent_page/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper "VC-Agent: An Interactive Agent for Customized Video Dataset Collection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VC-Agent, an interactive agent based on Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) designed to streamline the process of creating customized video datasets from the internet. The agent interacts with users in an iterative manner, refining user requirements through textual queries, confirmations, and comments on proposed video clips. The agent uses a novel filtering policy incorporating template-based acceptance and attribute-aware rejection mechanisms to iteratively improve the quality of the collected dataset. After a few rounds of human interaction, VC-Agent transitions to a fully automated mode for scaling up the dataset. The authors provide a new benchmark for personalized video dataset collection, and usability studies demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of the agent.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a well-defined system with several novel components. The interactive approach to dataset creation, guided by human feedback using an MLLM, is a significant departure from traditional, one-shot video retrieval methods. The filtering policy with template-based acceptance and attribute-aware rejection is a new contribution that adapts to user input. The creation of a personalized video collection benchmark is also valuable, given the lack of existing resources for this specific task.

*   **Significance:** Creating high-quality, task-specific video datasets is time-consuming and difficult. By automating much of this process, VC-Agent has the potential to reduce costs and facilitate research in areas dependent on specialized video data. The user studies demonstrate that the agent can effectively collect large video datasets and that the resulting data is of high quality. The proposed system can significantly improve the efficiency of video data collection for various applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined solution.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation with a new benchmark and user studies.
    *   Novel and well-integrated filtering policies using user feedback.
    *   The interactive nature of VC-Agent makes it adaptable to various complex requirements.
    *   Well-documented, including code and UI demo.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Computational cost compared to traditional video retrieval due to leveraging MLLMs.
    *   Dependence on MLLMs. Certain cases (realistic virtual scenes and recognizing movements) are not very successful. As the paper said, reliance on MLLMs is not the paper's central focus.
    *   While the new benchmark is helpful, the specific use cases may not be universally applicable, but it's certainly a great addition to a research space that doesn't have a benchmark.
    *   The follow-up survey showed one instance where VC-Agent underperformed LLAVA-OneVision for the pose estimation task. This is not a significant criticism, as it pertains to a particular task.
    * The paper focuses on a smaller number of users compared to other papers that focus on this specific research area.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research on automated dataset creation, especially for tasks requiring customized video data. The interactive approach and feedback mechanisms developed in VC-Agent can inspire similar systems for other data modalities and tasks. The benchmark provides a valuable tool for evaluating and comparing different approaches to personalized video collection.

**Overall:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of automated data collection by presenting a novel and effective system for creating customized video datasets. It addresses a real-world problem with a well-defined solution, comprehensive evaluation, and clear benefits. While some limitations exist, the overall impact of the paper is significant.

### Semantic Clustering of Civic Proposals: A Case Study on Brazil's National Participation Platform
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21292v1)
- **Authors**: Ronivaldo Ferreira, Guilherme da Silva, Carla Rocha, Gustavo Pinto
- **Abstract**: Promoting participation on digital platforms such as Brasil Participativo has emerged as a top priority for governments worldwide. However, due to the sheer volume of contributions, much of this engagement goes underutilized, as organizing it presents significant challenges: (1) manual classification is unfeasible at scale; (2) expert involvement is required; and (3) alignment with official taxonomies is necessary. In this paper, we introduce an approach that combines BERTopic with seed words and automatic validation by large language models. Initial results indicate that the generated topics are coherent and institutionally aligned, with minimal human effort. This methodology enables governments to transform large volumes of citizen input into actionable data for public policy.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Semantic Clustering of Civic Proposals: A Case Study on Brazil's National Participation Platform."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of organizing and analyzing large volumes of citizen contributions on digital participation platforms, using Brazil's "Brasil Participativo" as a case study. The authors propose a methodology combining BERTopic, seed words from the Vocabulário Controlado de Governo Eletrônico (VCGE), and automatic validation using large language models (LLMs). They aim to improve the coherence and institutional alignment of generated topics while minimizing manual effort. The results indicate that their approach generates coherent and institutionally aligned topics, demonstrating its potential for transforming citizen input into actionable data for public policy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a practical problem:** The paper tackles a significant challenge in e-governance – effectively processing and utilizing citizen input on large-scale digital platforms. This is a crucial area for improving democratic processes and policy formulation.
    *   **Methodologically sound:** The combination of BERTopic, seed words, and LLM validation is a sensible approach. BERTopic provides a good base for unsupervised topic modeling, the seed words help to ground the topics in an official taxonomy, and the LLM helps in validation and label generation reducing manual effort.
    *   **Clear experimental setup:** The paper clearly describes the experimental setup, including the dataset, the models used (BERTimbau-large, Gemma 3), and the evaluation metrics (coherence, diversity, ARI, NMI).
    *   **Empirical evidence:** The results demonstrate improved coherence and institutional alignment compared to a purely unsupervised approach. The tables and figures provide clear evidence to support the claims.
    *   **Focus on an important real-world case study:** Using the "Brasil Participativo" platform adds practical relevance and provides insights into the challenges and opportunities in the Brazilian context.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited novelty:** While the combination of techniques is sensible, the novelty might be limited. BERTopic, seed words, and LLMs are established tools, and simply combining them is not a groundbreaking contribution. The core methodological contribution is not significantly advanced.
    *   **Evaluation metrics:** The evaluation mainly focuses on traditional topic modeling metrics like coherence, diversity, ARI, and NMI. While these are useful, they don't directly measure the actual impact on public policy or decision-making, which is the ultimate goal. It relies heavily on extrinsic evaluation with labels provided by LLMs.
    *   **Lack of comparative baselines:** While comparing unsupervised and semi-supervised methods is helpful, the paper could benefit from comparing against other state-of-the-art topic modeling techniques or classification methods specifically designed for government documents.
    *   **Limited generalizability:** The paper focuses on the Brazilian context and the VCGE taxonomy. The results may not directly generalize to other countries with different languages, cultures, and institutional frameworks.
    *   **Limited analysis of the role of the LLM** - the paper fails to describe the effect of the LLM (other than as a labeller) which could provide much additional insight in qualitative analysis of the generated topics.
    *  **Lack of detail about the seed words** - Given that the seed words are a critical part of the model, it is crucial to explore different methods for selecting them as well as describe the exact words in use.

*   **Significance:**

The paper contributes to the practical application of NLP techniques in e-governance. It provides a working methodology for helping governments to process large quantities of public contributions to help make better policies. Its focus on institutional alignment is important for ensuring that citizen input is considered in a structured and meaningful way.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses an important practical problem with a reasonable methodology and demonstrates positive results. However, the limited novelty and lack of strong comparative baselines, and metrics that truly measure practical policy impact prevent a higher score. The approach is a sensible combination of existing techniques, but does not represent a significant advancement in the state of the art. The focus on the Brazilian context also limits the generalizability of the findings.

### Quantized Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21302v1)
- **Authors**: Weilun Feng, Haotong Qin, Mingqiang Wu, Chuanguang Yang, Yuqi Li, Xiangqi Li, Zhulin An, Libo Huang, Yulun Zhang, Michele Magno, Yongjun Xu
- **Abstract**: Learning-based 3D reconstruction models, represented by Visual Geometry Grounded Transformers (VGGTs), have made remarkable progress with the use of large-scale transformers. Their prohibitive computational and memory costs severely hinder real-world deployment. Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) has become a common practice for compressing and accelerating models. However, we empirically observe that PTQ faces unique obstacles when compressing billion-scale VGGTs: the data-independent special tokens induce heavy-tailed activation distributions, while the multi-view nature of 3D data makes calibration sample selection highly unstable. This paper proposes the first Quantization framework for VGGTs, namely QuantVGGT. This mainly relies on two technical contributions: First, we introduce Dual-Smoothed Fine-Grained Quantization, which integrates pre-global Hadamard rotation and post-local channel smoothing to mitigate heavy-tailed distributions and inter-channel variance robustly. Second, we design Noise-Filtered Diverse Sampling, which filters outliers via deep-layer statistics and constructs frame-aware diverse calibration clusters to ensure stable quantization ranges. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that QuantVGGT achieves the state-of-the-art results across different benchmarks and bit-width, surpassing the previous state-of-the-art generic quantization method with a great margin. We highlight that our 4-bit QuantVGGT can deliver a 3.7$\times$ memory reduction and 2.5$\times$ acceleration in real-hardware inference, while maintaining reconstruction accuracy above 98\% of its full-precision counterpart. This demonstrates the vast advantages and practicality of QuantVGGT in resource-constrained scenarios. Our code is released in https://github.com/wlfeng0509/QuantVGGT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Quantized Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QuantVGGT, a post-training quantization (PTQ) framework specifically designed for Visual Geometry Grounded Transformers (VGGTs). VGGTs, while powerful for 3D reconstruction tasks, suffer from high computational and memory demands. The authors identify two key challenges in quantizing VGGTs: 1) data-independent special tokens that cause skewed activation distributions, and 2) the inherent complexity and multi-view nature of 3D data making calibration sample selection unstable. QuantVGGT addresses these challenges with two main contributions:

1.  **Dual-Smoothed Fine-Grained Quantization (DSFQ):** A method that employs pre-global Hadamard rotation and post-local channel smoothing to mitigate heavy-tailed distributions and inter-channel variance.
2.  **Noise-Filtered Diverse Sampling (NFDS):** A calibration strategy that filters outliers using deep-layer statistics and constructs frame-aware diverse calibration clusters to ensure stable quantization ranges.

The paper demonstrates through experiments that QuantVGGT achieves state-of-the-art quantization results across different benchmarks and bit-widths, significantly reducing memory footprint and accelerating inference while maintaining reconstruction accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its targeted approach to quantizing a specific, large-scale 3D reconstruction transformer (VGGT). While PTQ and related techniques like smoothing and outlier filtering are not entirely new concepts, their application and adaptation to the unique characteristics of VGGT are novel.  The identification of VGGT-specific quantization challenges (data-independent tokens, multi-view calibration instability) is also a valuable contribution. The specific designs of DSFQ and NFDS tailored to VGGT's architecture and data are more than just a direct application of existing methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it addresses a critical practical problem: deploying large 3D reconstruction models in resource-constrained environments.  VGGTs are powerful but computationally expensive.  QuantVGGT offers a pathway to making these models more accessible and deployable.  The experimental results clearly demonstrate substantial memory reduction and speedup, showcasing the practicality of the approach.  The fact that it surpasses existing generic quantization techniques highlights the importance of model-specific quantization strategies.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Identification:** The paper clearly articulates the specific challenges associated with quantizing VGGTs.
    *   **Targeted Solutions:** The proposed DSFQ and NFDS are well-motivated and tailored to address the identified challenges.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experiments are comprehensive, covering various benchmarks, bit-widths, and ablation studies, demonstrating the effectiveness of QuantVGGT.
    *   **Practical Impact:** The paper offers a tangible solution for deploying large 3D reconstruction models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** While the techniques are adapted for VGGT, the paper may be somewhat specific to this particular architecture. It is not clear to what extent DSFQ and NFDS could be readily applied to other large 3D reconstruction models without significant adaptation.
    *   **Incremental Advance:** While the paper adapts established quantization techniques to VGGT's particular characteristics, it may not represent a *fundamental* leap in quantization research. While the combination of novel ideas is tailored towards VGGT, the framework may not be a disruptive one.
    *   **Reliance on VGGT:** Since the paper is directly contingent on the VGGT architecture, the paper's impact is reliant on the VGGT architecture remaining a prominent one in the field.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could influence the field in several ways:
    *   **Model-Specific Quantization:** It reinforces the importance of considering model-specific characteristics when designing quantization strategies.
    *   **3D Reconstruction Deployment:** It facilitates the deployment of large 3D reconstruction models in resource-constrained settings.
    *   **Future Quantization Research:** It may inspire further research into quantization techniques specifically tailored for other large-scale 3D models.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed solution to a practical problem (quantizing large 3D reconstruction models). The identified VGGT-specific challenges and the tailored solutions (DSFQ and NFDS) contribute to the novelty. The strong experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach. However, the paper is somewhat specific to VGGT and may not represent a fundamental breakthrough in quantization research *per se*. The impact is significant because it enables practical applications of these otherwise computationally-expensive models, however the paper's impact may be limited by how long the underlying VGGT model will remain relevant in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sycophancy Is Not One Thing: Causal Separation of Sycophantic Behaviors in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21305v1)
- **Authors**: Daniel Vennemeyer, Phan Anh Duong, Tiffany Zhan, Tianyu Jiang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) often exhibit sycophantic behaviors -- such as excessive agreement with or flattery of the user -- but it is unclear whether these behaviors arise from a single mechanism or multiple distinct processes. We decompose sycophancy into sycophantic agreement and sycophantic praise, contrasting both with genuine agreement. Using difference-in-means directions, activation additions, and subspace geometry across multiple models and datasets, we show that: (1) the three behaviors are encoded along distinct linear directions in latent space; (2) each behavior can be independently amplified or suppressed without affecting the others; and (3) their representational structure is consistent across model families and scales. These results suggest that sycophantic behaviors correspond to distinct, independently steerable representations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Sycophancy Is Not One Thing: Causal Separation of Sycophantic Behaviors in LLMs" investigates whether sycophancy in large language models (LLMs) is a unified phenomenon or arises from distinct underlying mechanisms.  The authors decompose sycophancy into sycophantic agreement (SYA) and sycophantic praise (SYPR), contrasting them with genuine agreement (GA). They use difference-in-means (DiffMean) directions, activation additions, and subspace geometry across multiple models and datasets to demonstrate that these three behaviors are encoded along distinct linear directions in the latent space.  Furthermore, they show that each behavior can be independently amplified or suppressed without affecting the others, and their representational structure remains consistent across different model families and scales.  The findings suggest that sycophantic behaviors correspond to distinct, independently steerable representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's central claim – that sycophancy isn't a monolithic behavior but comprises separable components – constitutes a significant advance over prior work. Previous studies have largely treated sycophancy as a single, cohesive entity, or analyzed subtypes without explicitly testing the hypothesis of functional separability. The authors' approach of decomposing sycophancy and demonstrating causal independence through steering and ablation is novel and insightful.
*   **Significance:**  The paper's findings have important implications for mitigating sycophancy in LLMs. By demonstrating that sycophantic agreement and praise can be independently controlled, the authors suggest that more targeted interventions are possible.  This is crucial, as blunt mitigation strategies risk inadvertently harming beneficial behaviors like honesty and alignment with ground truth. The work provides both conceptual clarity and practical tools for evaluating and addressing harmful deference without sacrificing desirable responsiveness. The replication of results across different model architectures and scales further enhances the significance of the work, suggesting that the observed patterns are generalizable.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined behaviors:** The authors provide clear operational definitions of SYA, SYPR, and GA, minimizing ambiguity and allowing for rigorous analysis.
    *   **Carefully constructed datasets:** The use of synthetic datasets allows for systematic variation of relevant factors (agreement vs. disagreement, praise vs. neutral), ensuring that observed differences reflect behavioral distinctions rather than dataset artifacts.  The sycophantic praise augmentation and inclusion of control cases are also commendable.
    *   **Robust methodology:** The combination of DiffMean directions, activation additions, subspace geometry, and ablation studies provides strong evidence for the separability of the behaviors. The use of multiple models and datasets further strengthens the results.
    *   **Clear and convincing results:** The results are presented clearly and are well-supported by the experimental evidence. The figures and tables are informative and easy to interpret.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Datasets:** While the synthetic datasets provide careful controls, the paper could have been strengthened if the causal interventions are applied to diverse set of "real-world" user conversations. Although the study showed this to some extent with the TruthfulQA dataset, diverse user conversation data and its inherent complexity can have a significant influence on the proposed results.
    *   **Limited scope of sycophancy:** The paper focuses on sycophantic agreement and praise, which are relatively narrow aspects of sycophancy. Future work could explore other dimensions of sycophancy, such as social sycophancy (emotional validation, framing acceptance) and mimicry.
*   **Potential Influence:** This work could significantly shift how researchers and practitioners conceptualize and address sycophancy. It opens new avenues for developing more precise and effective mitigation strategies. Future work building on this foundation could lead to safer and more reliable LLMs. The paper also contributes to the broader understanding of how social behaviors are encoded and controlled in LLMs.

**Overall Assessment:** This paper makes a strong contribution to the field by providing compelling evidence that sycophancy is not a monolithic phenomenon. Its findings have significant implications for the development of more effective mitigation strategies and for improving the alignment of LLMs. While there are some limitations, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a significant advance over previous work.
